\subsection{Dephasing-Based First-Principles Candidate for \texorpdfstring{$t_{\rm coh}$}{tcoh}}
\label{app:tcoh_candidate}
To close the finite-time module with the same action-derived spectral chain, we test the dephasing candidate
\begin{equation}
\Delta\omega_{12}(D)\equiv \omega_2(D)-\omega_1(D),\qquad
t_{\rm coh}^{\rm(deph)}(D)\equiv \frac{\pi}{\Delta\omega_{12}(D)},
\label{eq:tcoh_dephasing}
\end{equation}
where $(\omega_1,\omega_2)$ are extracted from the bound-state convention $E_n=\omega_n^2-m_0^2<0$ in the same 1D on-axis operator chain as Appendix~\ref{app:Veff}. Detailed coarse/mid/fine values and implementation provenance are retained in the artifact registry. On the benchmark set $D=\{6,12,18\}$, the maximum non-fine relative deviation is
\begin{equation}
\max\!\left(\frac{|t_{\rm coh}-t_{\rm coh}^{\rm(fine)}|}{|t_{\rm coh}^{\rm(fine)}|}\right)=8.86\times10^{-3},
\end{equation}
which is numerically stable at the sub-$10^{-2}$ level for this benchmark set.
The original profile-impact scan compared constant $t_{\rm coh}=1$, raw Eq.~\eqref{eq:tcoh_dephasing}, and the capped variant $\min(t_{\rm coh}^{\rm(deph)},10^4)$ in a legacy diagnostic chain.  For the current action-derived baseline we instead use the registered impact gate, which holds the present $g_N$, localized $\chi_N^{(LR)}$, action-derived $\tilde A_\ell(D)$, and EFT/Wilson $H\to\mu\mu$ map fixed while changing only the $t_{\rm coh}$ policy.  On the D60/E21 map the constant-input baseline gives
\begin{equation}
f(\mathcal R_3\ge0.90)=0.926984,\qquad
f(\mathcal R_3\ge0.95)=0.925397,\qquad
f(\chi^2_{\mu\mu}\le4)=0.150000.
\end{equation}
The dephasing profile has \(t_{\rm coh}^{\rm(deph)}(D)\in[40.5692,3.47174\times10^6]\) on the scanned window and saturates the kinetic competition:
\begin{equation}
f(\mathcal R_3\ge0.90)=1.000000,\qquad
f(\mathcal R_3\ge0.95)=1.000000,\qquad
f(\chi^2_{\mu\mu}\le4)=0.133333.
\end{equation}
Relative to the constant-input baseline, the raw and \(10^4\)-capped profiles both have acceptance mismatch \(0.016667\), winner mismatch \(0.005556\), \(p95|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|=4.95503\), and \(p95|\Delta\mathcal R_3|=0.96245\) on D60/E21.  The prolate-mesh cross-check profile gives the same decision within rounding.  Therefore Eq.~\eqref{eq:tcoh_dephasing} is a useful first-principles diagnostic of the dephasing scale, but it is not promoted into the baseline reported figures.  The registered summary certificates are indexed in the artifact registry.

\subsection{Splitting-Prefactor First-Principles Candidate for \texorpdfstring{$\eta$}{eta}}
\label{app:eta_candidate}
To test a geometry-closed replacement direction for the overlap amplitude in Eq.~\eqref{eq:rN}, we use the same action-derived quantities $(\Delta E_{12},S_1)$ and define the splitting prefactor
\begin{equation}
\Delta E_{12}(D)\approx A_{12}(D)\,\ee^{-S_1(D)},\qquad
A_{12}(D)\equiv \Delta E_{12}(D)\,\ee^{S_1(D)}.
\end{equation}
With a reference point $D_{\rm ref}=12$, we define two dimensionless candidates:
\begin{equation}
\eta_{\rm amp}(D)\equiv \frac{A_{12}(D)}{A_{12}(D_{\rm ref})},\qquad
\eta_{\rm prob}(D)\equiv \eta_{\rm amp}(D)^2.
\label{eq:eta_pref_candidates}
\end{equation}
Detailed coarse/mid/fine values and implementation provenance are retained in the artifact registry. On the same $D=\{6,12,18\}$ benchmark set, we obtain
\begin{equation}
\max\!\left(\frac{|\eta_{\rm amp}-\eta_{\rm amp}^{\rm(fine)}|}{|\eta_{\rm amp}^{\rm(fine)}|}\right)=1.08\times10^{-2},\qquad
\max\!\left(\frac{|\eta_{\rm prob}-\eta_{\rm prob}^{\rm(fine)}|}{|\eta_{\rm prob}^{\rm(fine)}|}\right)=2.18\times10^{-2},
\end{equation}
and the fine full-scan profile over $D=4,\dots,20$ gives $\eta_{\rm amp}\in[0.9996,1.1695]$ and $\eta_{\rm prob}\in[0.9992,1.3677]$.  For the current action-derived baseline, the registered impact gate holds the present $g_N$, localized $\chi_N^{(LR)}$, action-derived $\tilde A_\ell(D)$, $t_{\rm coh}=1$, and EFT/Wilson $H\to\mu\mu$ map fixed while changing only the $\eta_{\rm eff}$ policy.  On the D60/E21 map, the scanned baseline gives
\begin{equation}
f(\mathcal R_3\ge0.90)=0.926984,\qquad
f(\mathcal R_3\ge0.95)=0.925397,\qquad
f(\chi^2_{\mu\mu}\le4)=0.150000.
\end{equation}
The amplitude-scaled branch \(\eta_{\rm eff}=\eta\,\eta_{\rm amp}(D)\) is map-safe:
\begin{equation}
f(\mathcal R_3\ge0.90)=0.926984,\qquad
f(\mathcal R_3\ge0.95)=0.926190,\qquad
f(\chi^2_{\mu\mu}\le4)=0.150000,
\end{equation}
with acceptance mismatch \(0\), winner mismatch \(0\), \(p95|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|=9.7896\times10^{-3}\), and \(p95|\Delta\mathcal R_3|=3.2316\times10^{-5}\).  The probability-scaled branch \(\eta_{\rm eff}=\eta\,\eta_{\rm prob}(D)\) is also map-safe, with the same headline fractions, acceptance mismatch \(0\), winner mismatch \(0\), \(p95|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|=2.0734\times10^{-2}\), and \(p95|\Delta\mathcal R_3|=6.8392\times10^{-5}\).  Therefore the profile-scaled \(\eta_{\rm fp}\) branches are adoption-safe under the current impact gate, with \(\eta_{\rm amp}\) the conservative amplitude-level choice.  By contrast, the fully closed branches \(\eta_{\rm eff}=\eta_{\rm amp}(D)\) or \(\eta_{\rm prob}(D)\) are kept diagnostic-only: they preserve the \(H\to\mu\mu\) accepted fraction but collapse the scanned $\eta$ axis and produce winner mismatch \(0.005556\).  The registered summary certificates are indexed in the artifact registry.

\subsection{Action-Derived First-Principles Candidate for Barrier-Leakage Channel Scaling}
\label{app:superrad_candidate}
To test a geometry-closed replacement direction for the channel normalization in Eq.~\eqref{eq:Gamma_channel}, we keep the same 1D action-derived chain and define channel-resolved barrier actions
\begin{equation}
U_\ell(z;D)\equiv U(z;D)+\frac{\ell(\ell+1)}{z^2+\varepsilon^2},\qquad
S_{N,\ell}(D)\equiv\int_{z_-}^{z_+}\dd z\,\sqrt{U_\ell(z;D)-E_N(D)},
\end{equation}
with $E_N=\omega_N^2-m_0^2$ in bound-state convention.
We then define geometry-driven channel rates
\begin{equation}
\Gamma_{N,\ell}^{\rm(geo)}(D)\equiv \omega_N(D)\,e^{-2S_{N,\ell}(D)},
\end{equation}
and infer effective channel normalizations by matching to Eq.~\eqref{eq:Gamma_channel}:
\begin{equation}
A_\ell^{\rm(fp)}(D;N)\equiv
\frac{\Gamma_{N,\ell}^{\rm(geo)}(D)}{\omega_N(D)\,(\omega_N(D)M_*)^{4\ell+4}}.
\end{equation}
Using $(D_{\rm ref},N_{\rm ref})=(12,2)$, we define profile factors
\begin{equation}
\tilde A_\ell(D)\equiv \frac{A_\ell^{\rm(fp)}(D;N_{\rm ref})}{A_\ell^{\rm(fp)}(D_{\rm ref};N_{\rm ref})}.
\label{eq:superrad_A_profile}
\end{equation}
Detailed coarse/mid/fine values and implementation provenance are retained in the artifact registry. On the $D=\{6,12,18\}$ benchmark set, the maximum non-fine relative deviations are
\begin{equation}
\max\!\left(\frac{|\tilde A_1-\tilde A_1^{\rm(fine)}|}{|\tilde A_1^{\rm(fine)}|}\right)=3.22\times10^{-2},\quad
\max\!\left(\frac{|\tilde A_2-\tilde A_2^{\rm(fine)}|}{|\tilde A_2^{\rm(fine)}|}\right)=3.78\times10^{-2}.
\end{equation}
The fine full-scan profile over $D=4,\dots,20$ yields $\tilde A_1\in[8.54\times10^{-6},\,4.10\times10^5]$ and $\tilde A_2\in[3.95\times10^{-6},\,4.92\times10^6]$, indicating a very stiff $D$-dependence in this preliminary closure.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Proposition A1 (localized channel-resolved \(A_\ell(D,\eta,N)\) tensor).}
In the rank-2 kinetic closure used in the scan, the scanned overlap amplitude \(\eta\) is a formation multiplier applied after the channel rates are formed.  Therefore the fully localized barrier normalization is the tensor
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
A_\ell^{\rm loc}(D,\eta,N)
&\equiv
\frac{\Gamma_{N,\ell}^{\rm loc}(D,\eta)}
{\eta_{\rm eff}(D,\eta)\,\omega_N(D)\,(\omega_N(D)M_*)^{4\ell+4}}\\
&=
\frac{\Gamma_{N,\ell}^{\rm(geo)}(D)}
{\omega_N(D)\,(\omega_N(D)M_*)^{4\ell+4}}
=
\frac{e^{-2S_{N,\ell}(D)}}{(\omega_N(D)M_*)^{4\ell+4}},
\end{aligned}
\label{eq:Aell_local_tensor}
\end{equation}
whenever \(\eta_{\rm eff}(D,\eta)>0\).  Hence
\begin{equation}
\partial_\eta A_\ell^{\rm loc}(D,\eta,N)=0,
\qquad
\Gamma_{N,\ell}^{\rm loc}(D,\eta)
=\eta_{\rm eff}(D,\eta)\,
A_\ell^{\rm loc}(D,\eta,N)\,\omega_N(D)(\omega_N(D)M_*)^{4\ell+4}.
\label{eq:Aell_eta_separable}
\end{equation}
The old profile \(\tilde A_\ell(D)\) in Eq.~\eqref{eq:superrad_A_profile} is therefore precisely the \(N=N_{\rm ref}=2\) slice of a localized \((D,\eta,N,\ell)\) tensor, not a separate family ansatz.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Corollary A1 (extraction status and remaining gate).}
The \(\eta\)-axis in the channel normalization is now an audited separability axis rather than an extra fit direction.  The production-level choice is not a new family search: one either keeps the current \(N_{\rm ref}=2\) profile slice in the global scan, or promotes the same tensor lookup subject to the bound-sector policy in Eq.~\eqref{eq:Aell_bound_sector_policy}.

\medskip\noindent\emph{Numerical audit.}
The registered executable extractor reuses the same one-dimensional action-derived operator chain as the baseline barrier-prefactor extractor.  On the full integer scan grid \(D=4,\dots,20\), with \(\eta\in\{0.25,1,4\}\), \(N\in\{1,2,3\}\), and \(\ell\in\{1,2\}\), it produces \(306\) tensor rows with all WKB actions finite and
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
\max_{D,N,\ell}\Bigl[\max_{\eta} A_\ell^{\rm loc}(D,\eta,N)
-\min_{\eta} A_\ell^{\rm loc}(D,\eta,N)\Bigr]&=0,\\
\max_{D,N,\ell}\frac{\Delta_\eta A_\ell^{\rm loc}}{\max_\eta|A_\ell^{\rm loc}|}&=0.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Using \((D_{\rm ref},N_{\rm ref})=(12,2)\), the tensor profile ranges are
\begin{equation}
\tilde A_1^{\rm loc}\in[8.54306\times10^{-6},\,7.16976\times10^5],
\qquad
\tilde A_2^{\rm loc}\in[3.95285\times10^{-6},\,1.46257\times10^7],
\end{equation}
where the larger upper endpoints include the \(N=1\) slice at small \(D\).  The maximum \(N\)-shape deviations relative to the same-\(D\), \(N_{\rm ref}=2\) slice are
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
\max|\log_{10}(A_1(D,N)/A_1(D,N_{\rm ref}))|&=5.326664,\\
\max|\log_{10}(A_2(D,N)/A_2(D,N_{\rm ref}))|&=3.461295.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
The \(N=3\) rows are kept in the tensor for diagnostic continuity but are marked with \(E_3>0\) on this window, so they are not a new bound-layer claim and remain consistent with Proposition N1 / Corollary N1.  The registered summary certificates are indexed in the artifact registry.

\medskip\noindent\emph{Scan-impact gate.}
The strict tensor lookup has also been wired into the library under the non-default modes \(\texttt{action\_tensor}\) and \(\texttt{action\_tensor\_grid\_strict}\), with \(N>N_{\rm max}^{\rm tensor}\) conservatively falling back to the \(N_{\rm ref}=2\) profile slice rather than opening a high-\(N\) channel family.  The registered impact gate builds a D60-aligned tensor, includes the required reference knots \(D=10,12\), and compares the strict tensor branch against the current \(N_{\rm ref}=2\) action-grid profile on the D60/E21 map.  The profile baseline gives
\begin{equation}
f(\mathcal R_3\ge0.90)=0.926984,\qquad
f(\chi^2_{\mu\mu}\le4)=0.150000,\qquad
\chi^2_{\rm best}=5.0965\times10^{-4},
\end{equation}
while the strict tensor branch gives
\begin{equation}
f(\mathcal R_3\ge0.90)=1.000000,\qquad
f(\chi^2_{\mu\mu}\le4)=0.033333,\qquad
\chi^2_{\rm best}=0.602237.
\end{equation}
The tensor-vs-profile comparison has acceptance mismatch \(0.116667\), winner mismatch \(0.633333\), \(p95|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|=2.65067\times10^3\), and \(\max|\Delta\mathcal R_3|=1\).  The failure is localized to the physical-domain policy, not to \(\eta\)-separability: the \(N=3\) tensor rows are positive-proxy rows on this window and should not be read as bound-channel normalizations.

We therefore also test the bound-sector policy
\begin{equation}
A_{\ell}^{\rm policy}(D,N)=
\begin{cases}
A_{\ell}^{\rm loc}(D,N), & b(D,N)=1,\\
A_{\ell}^{\rm loc}(D,N_{\rm ref}), & b(D,N)=0,
\end{cases}
\qquad N_{\rm ref}=2,
\label{eq:Aell_bound_sector_policy}
\end{equation}
where \(b(D,N)\) is the audited \(\texttt{bound\_proxy}\) flag, equivalently the finite-box binding-domain indicator for this channel export.  In code this is the same tensor mode with the policy flag \(\texttt{gamma\_tensor\_bound\_policy=bound\_only\_fallback}\); it is not a new channel-normalization family.  On the same D60/E21 gate it gives
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
f(\mathcal R_3\ge0.90)&=0.926984,&
f(\mathcal R_3\ge0.95)&=0.926190,\\
f(\chi^2_{\mu\mu}\le4)&=0.150000,&
\chi^2_{\rm best}&=5.2433\times10^{-4}.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Against the current profile baseline, this policy branch has acceptance mismatch \(0\), winner mismatch \(0\), \(p95|\Delta\mu_{\mu\mu}|=1.10319\times10^{-1}\), \(\max|\Delta\mathcal R_3|=2.799\times10^{-3}\), and \(p95|\Delta\mathcal R_3|=5.815\times10^{-4}\).  Thus the all-valid tensor lookup is retained only as an extraction stress test, while the physically admissible tensor promotion route is the bound-sector fallback policy in Eq.~\eqref{eq:Aell_bound_sector_policy}.  The registered summary certificates are indexed in the artifact registry.

\paragraph{Reviewer-facing compression of the static-width audit chain.}
The detailed S1--S25 propositions record provenance and failure modes.  The appendix-level reading is the following five-step certificate chain.

\paragraph{CAP, local Robin, ECS, and finite Whittaker roots.}
\emph{Theorem status:} diagnostic infrastructure only; no physical static-width promotion is taken from these branches.
\emph{Certificate artifacts:} indexed in the artifact registry under the CAP, exact-DtN, ECS, and branch-isolation static-width entries.
\emph{Numerical conclusion:} CAP is absorber-linear and finite roots are locally isolated but exterior-parameter sensitive, so branch picking is not the theorem.

\paragraph{S9--S15 parent-lift localization.}
\emph{Theorem status:} the obstruction is quantified as parent displacement and common-box failure, not local zero existence.
\emph{Certificate artifacts:} the parent-lift, tail-conditioning, projective-transfer, Jost-product, contour-Jost, and R-coherence summaries.
\emph{Numerical conclusion:} endpoint Jost-product margins pass while common R-box intersections fail; square-cover factors remain about 36--37 in the benchmark groups.

\paragraph{S16--S21 R-flow bottleneck.}
\emph{Theorem status:} the live gate narrows to the early R-flow piece and then closes on parent-scale tubes.
\emph{Certificate artifacts:} the R-flow, large-tube Rouche, adapted-flow, and curved-flowbox audits.
\emph{Numerical conclusion:} the final \(60\to80\) parity pair closes with max best ratio \(0.918494\), median \(0.905757\), and derivative floor \(97.09324\).

\paragraph{S22--S24 slab/interval promotion.}
\emph{Theorem status:} the 5\% reserve is converted into a Cauchy/slab derivative remainder and then a parity-uniform interval theorem.
\emph{Certificate artifacts:} the slab-envelope, Cauchy/slab, and interval-promotion summaries under the \texttt{d6rc400\_r60\_r80\_c640} prefix.
\emph{Numerical conclusion:} all \(64/64\) slab inequalities pass, with max reserve usage \(0.793969\) and max interval integrated ratio \(0.954314\).

\paragraph{S25 adopted certificate.}
\emph{Theorem status:} the local strict static-width theorem is adopted on \(D=6,R_C=400,R=60\to80,c=640\), while \(\Gamma_{N,\ell}^{(\rm geo)}\) remains fallback outside.
\emph{Certificate artifacts:} indexed in the artifact registry under the S25 special-interval static-width certificate.
\emph{Numerical conclusion:} \(2/2\) parity branches close, with max guarded-direct/S24 ratio \(0.888891\) and max direct integrated ratio \(0.938387\).

At current scan resolution, Table~\ref{tab:late_fp_profile_summary} shows the status of the three first-principles profile candidates.  The $t_{\rm coh}$ profile is now a diagnostic-only negative for baseline promotion on the current action-derived map, the profile-scaled $\eta$ candidates are adoption-safe with zero acceptance/winner flips, and replacing the legacy constant-$A_\ell$ reference by the action-derived profile induces non-negligible shifts in $\mathcal{R}_3$ coverage. In the updated global scans of this manuscript, Eq.~\eqref{eq:superrad_A_profile} is used in baseline mode, and $(A_1,A_2)=(1,1)$ is retained only as a comparator.

\section{Ansatz Ablation Study}
\label{app:ablation}

To demonstrate that the first-three-layer occupancy concentration is robust against a visible ansatz change, we compare the baseline EFT-operator-normalized $B_N(D)$ profile (this work) against the alternative ``inverse Yukawa'' ansatz explored in earlier versions.

\subsection{Inverse Yukawa Ansatz (Ablation A)}
The inverse Yukawa ansatz sets $B_N^{\rm (inv)} \propto 1/\sum_{f \in \text{Gen } N} y_f$, yielding:
\begin{equation}
B_1^{\rm (inv)} \approx 10^5, \quad B_2^{\rm (inv)} \approx 125, \quad B_3^{\rm (inv)} = 1.
\end{equation}
This massive hierarchy ($B_1 \gg B_2 \gg B_3$) directly encodes the answer by over-enhancing layer 1. While it can produce $\mathcal{R}_3 > 95\%$, the mechanism is less transparent: the visibility factor, rather than the entropic/kinetic competition, drives the result.

\subsection{Comparison}
\begin{itemize}
    \item \textbf{Baseline (EFT-operator-normalized profile)}: $B_N(D)$ remains smooth and bounded across the scanned range, with shape inherited from tracked microcanonical overlap extraction and operator normalization. The first-three-layer occupancy concentration is produced by the $g_N$--$\Gamma_N$ competition modulated by these profile differences.
    \item \textbf{Ablation A (Inverse Yukawa)}: $B_1 \gg B_2 \gg B_3$. Layer 1 is artificially boosted; the selection mechanism is less falsifiable.
\end{itemize}

We recommend the baseline as the primary closure due to its greater transparency and falsifiability.
